| R65 | **§9 #26 history as a record — the third owner-picked batch, built to four answers asked first:** doses the app never fired become **Missed** rows on launch and boot (over the alternative of leaving them absent); the day-end auto-confirm was declined in favour of keeping *Taken at fire time* as the only automatic answer; a deleted medicine is **archived, not cascaded** — the reversal of `DoseEventDao.kt`'s recorded cascade, argued here; and the as-needed ceiling **warns with a confirm** at the limit, never refuses. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece 1** (`e24e289`) — the data layer, **DB v7 → v8** (`MigrationFrom7To8Test`): `dose_events.notes` and a `DELETED` status excluded from every read that feeds adherence, History, the exports, the projection and the resolved anchor — the slot stays *claimed*, so a deleted dose is never re-materialised by the alarm path (the point of soft over hard); `reminders.instructions` and `dosesRemaining` (MT-07's course: counts down on Taken only, floored at zero, and `SchedulingEngine` returns no next occurrence at zero — the end condition beside `activeUntil`, as MT finding 3 asked); `medicines.archived` with `prnMaxDoses`/`prnWindowHours`; a `medicine_notes` table cascading with its medicine. `editDoseAmount` on a Taken dose now moves stock by the difference and re-writes the before/after pair (F.3 PT-F1 — R61 fixed the Missed→Taken order, this fixes the edit-afterwards); `reopen` gives stock back, clears the pair and re-arms through the coordinator; `reschedule` moves a pending slot and asks for a one-off alarm there (`onDoseRescheduled`, the snooze slot re-used); `archive` pauses the reminders and cancels their alarms rather than deleting them — a test caught that deleting them cascades to `dose_events`, which is the whole cascade the owner chose to keep history from. Eight JVM cases over a real in-memory Room. **Piece 2** (`edb6ff1`): `MissedDoseBackfiller` (DS-01, Dosage's `addMissedItems`) projects every history-independent reminder (FixedTime, ON_REMINDED intervals, windows — an ON_RESOLVED chain and a LinkedFollowing child depend on answers nobody gave, so they get no invented slots) from the last instant the app is known to have run (`app_last_seen_epoch_millis`, stamped on launch, boot and every alarm fire) to a minute ago, capped at **60 days**, through the same idempotent materialisation the alarm uses, so a slot the alarm did reach is never doubled; nothing is notified from here — R64's reposter keeps its own day-long window. Instructions go on the notification body before the stock count (MT-06), with `BigTextStyle` so three parts are readable. Three cases. **Piece 3** (`ffa8fec`) — the UI: the dose dialog gains a note, *Return to due* (re-posts the notification; medTimer RERAISE), *Move* (day + time; RESCHEDULE) and a two-tap soft *Delete* — the test that failed was the delete button below the fold of a phone-height dialog, which Robolectric's clipped touch injection refuses exactly as a thumb would; the reminder form gains the instructions line and "a set number of doses"; the medicine form the as-needed limit; the detail screen a notes journal (MT-04: three newest, show-all, edit and delete from the entry). Today lists as-needed medicines — those with no dosing reminder switched on — as standing rows with the platform's own relative time since the last dose and a Log chip (DS-10, PT-02/03); a projected slot can be taken early ("Take now", the row itself stays a non-button); the reminder's instructions sit on the row. The Log dose dialog backdates to any day and time (HM-26/MT-13), shows headroom against the ceiling as "N of M in the last H h · room again at T" (`prnHeadroom`, pure: room returns when the (taken−max+1)-th oldest dose leaves the window) and at the ceiling asks once and logs anyway on a yes. Settings › Data lists archived medicines with Restore and *Delete for good* — the only place the cascade is still reachable, behind a confirm that says the history goes too. The History export asks for a range preset (30 / 90 days, this year, everything) and an optional medicine before the file picker and fetches that scope once (`getInRangeOnce`) rather than exporting the visible window (MK-03/DS-F2); the CSV gains a `Notes` column, quoted like the name — a column change on the R61 shape. **Not done, by design:** the early-take window (MT-09) — "Take now" on any projected slot today covers the case without a per-reminder minutes field; amount-before-confirm on Today (DS-11) — the dialog already edits the amount after; DS-02's day-end auto-confirm, declined. **Left open:** the as-needed row's "last dose" reads any non-refill row, so an ad-hoc Skipped counts as a dose; the headroom counts Taken by `resolvedAt`, so a backdated log lands where it was taken, which is right, but a dose Taken with no `resolvedAt` (none exist after v3) would not count. Owed a device look: the notes journal on a small screen, the export dialog's chip row. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **287 tests / 0 failures** (259 → 287), `lintDebug` green after one `PluralsCandidate` reword (fixed, not baselined, per CLAUDE.md); device suite **6/0/0** on LE2123 over the v8 migration, debug build reinstalled |  |
| R66 | **§9 #27 medicine cabinet — Appendix F.5 group 4, built to seven answers asked first:** the dosage form is the **icon picker** (each option carries a default unit, no column) over a separate catalogue; **tags become the kits** (group-by-tag in the sort menu, a persisted tag filter with a badge) over a new Kit entity; expiry is **month/year by default with an opened-date and shelf life** whose earlier date is the one that counts; **strength and Rx number** are the two fields adopted from HM-14/DT-10 (alias, composition, recommendations declined); search is a **live in-memory filter**, not FTS; low stock gets **the tab badge and a summary notification** — the one place the owner chose the larger option; grouping lives in the sort menu and the filter survives a restart. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece 1** (`35877a7`) — **DB v8 → v9** (`MIGRATION_8_9`, schema 9 exported, `MigrationFrom8To9Test`): `medicines.strength`, `rxNumber`, `openedDateEpochDay`, `useWithinDaysAfterOpening`, all nullable, the last two on the embedded `StockInfo`. `StockInfo.effectiveExpirationEpochDay` — a computed property, not a column, so neither Room nor the backup format carries a derived value — is the earlier of the printed expiry and opened + shelf life, and the four reads that used to touch `expirationDateEpochDay` (the engine's alert arming, the receiver's still-due check, the card's expired state, the expiry sort) all moved to it: HM-10 stored an opened date and did nothing with it; here eye drops opened in March expire in April whatever the box says, and the engine test pins that the alert arms on that date. `MedicineIconOption.defaultUnit` (tablet → tablet, drops → drop, inhaler → puff, …; the descriptive forms carry none) fills a blank unit field and **never overwrites a typed one** (`unitAfterPickingForm`, pure) — HM-05's `DrugType.doseType` re-thought without a schema change. `MonthYearField`: a month column and a year column, one radio node per option, storing the month's **last day** so "expired" flips on the first of the month after, which is what "EXP 03/2027" means; the schema keeps day granularity, an "Exact day" switch keeps the old picker, and a stored non-month-end day displays as a full date so switching modes never rounds a chosen date; leap February tested. Strength under the name on the card and the detail, and between name and form on the emergency card ("Sertraline 50 mg (tablets)" — still no dose amount, the exporter's rule); Rx in a `SelectionContainer` on the detail. Two lint rounds: `NonObservableLocale` on the month names — `LocalConfiguration.current.locales[0]`, bare, as `HistoryCalendarView` already does; even a `?: Locale.getDefault()` fallback trips it. **Piece 2** (`0f6a801`): `MedicinesSort.GROUP_BY_TAG` — `groupByTag` (pure) makes one sticky-headed section per tag in collation order, a two-tag medicine **under both** (a kit is where a medicine can be found, not the one place it is — which is why the grouped list keys on `tag/id` and hides the drag handle: an item that appears twice has no single index to move), the untagged bucket last, manual order inside. `filterEntries` (pure): the query matches case-insensitively in name, strength, indication or a tag name; selected tags pass a medicine carrying **any** of them; blank passes all. The filter is persisted **by tag name** (tags are keyed by name on the form; a renamed or deleted tag simply stops matching) beside the sort key and hide-empty; the search is not persisted — a search is a moment, a filter is a view. `MedicinesFilterBar` is its own composable: the field carries "Search medicines" as its content description, one node per chip, the badge count as a plural. An emptied list says "Nothing matches", not "No medicines". **Piece 3** (`021e51d`): `stockAttentionCount` (pure) — tracked and empty, tracked and at or below an *enabled* low-stock threshold, or expired by the effective date, counted per medicine not per reason — the cards' own predicates, so the number on the tab can never disagree with the cards behind it; `BadgedBox` on the Medicines `NavigationBarItem`, hidden at zero, its content description a plural. Every stock alert joins `STOCK_GROUP_KEY` with a delete intent; from two showing, `updateStockSummary()` posts a silent `InboxStyle` summary naming them, rebuilt after every post, every refill and every swipe, gone below two — R64's `updateGroupSummary` shape on the alerts' negative ids; the per-medicine cards stay because they carry Refill (Appendix F called them the better data). **Not done, by design:** HM-04 photos and MT-02 versioning (L, parked as F.5 says); alias/composition/recommendations (declined); per-tag user ordering (sections sort by name); FTS (declined; the cabinet is tens of rows). **Left open:** the summary's count is only as fresh as the last event — a notification the OS drops without a delete broadcast leaves it one high until the next post; the month picker's year range is fixed at last year to ten ahead. Owed a device look: the month/year picker feel, sticky headers on the six palettes, the badge in dark mode, plus the R59/R63/R65 list. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **311 tests / 0 failures** (287 → 311), `lintDebug` green after one `PluralsCandidate` conversion and the locale fix; device suite **6/0/0** on LE2123 over the v9 migration (twice: after piece 1 and after piece 3's receiver change), debug build reinstalled |  |
| R67 | **§9 #28 Stage C — the app's own language, the time format, and the first translation (Appendix F.5 group 5), built to four answers asked first:** Italian, **in this batch**, finished with the `correttore-italiano` skill; an **in-app list on every version, registered with the system on Android 13+**; a time format of **Follow the phone / 12-hour / 24-hour, default Follow the phone**, no date-pattern menu; **neither** start page (HM-33) nor AMOLED (HM-34) pulled in. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece 1** (`18830ec`): `AppLocale` — one value, a BCP-47 tag or null for the system. On 13+ it is `LocaleManager.setApplicationLocales`, which the framework persists and applies process-wide (activities recreate; receivers, notification builders and Glance already speak the language), and `generateLocaleConfig` with `resources.properties` (`unqualifiedResLocale=en-US`, F-31) registers the shipped languages so Android's per-app language page lists exactly them — verified in the merged manifest, not added by hand. On 11–12 (minSdk 30) the Application and the three activities wrap their base context from a synchronous `SharedPreferences` (DataStore does not exist yet in `attachBaseContext`) and move `Locale.getDefault()` with it, so screens, notifications and every formatter follow; Glance widgets stay in the system language there — the one known limit. The picker is a radio list, each language **named in itself** ("Italiano", the platform convention, so a person who cannot read the current language still finds theirs), the two pseudolocales joining on a debuggable build; Language and Time format apply on the spot, unlike the palette and theme above them which wait for Save, because a language change recreates the screen. **The 24-hour defect** (MT-20's real content): `DateTimeFormatter.ofLocalizedTime(SHORT)` decides 12/24 from the locale alone, so a US-locale phone set to 24-hour read "8:05 PM" here. `LocaleFormats.time` is a skeleton now — `Hm`, `hm`, or the locale's own `jm` — fed by the setting (`hour24Override`) and by `DateFormat.is24HourFormat` (`systemHour24`), both installed by the container the way `useAlarmClock` is; the notification body's bare formatter moved to it. Lint's `AppBundleLocaleChanges` demanded `bundle { language { enableSplit = false } }` — moot for a sideloaded APK (§1), right for a bundle. **Piece 2** (`2a8dbc6`): `values-it/strings.xml`, **504 strings and 9 plurals**, written key by key from the English and generated in the English key order, with the skill's "testo tradotto verso l'italiano" pass integrated rather than appended: registro tecnico/UI, imperatives for actions (Salva, Annulla, Prendi, Salta), no calques ("aderenza" not "adesione", "scorta" not "stock", "rinvia" not "posticipa" for snooze), typographic apostrophes so nothing needs escaping, en dashes with spaces for the incisi; blood group "0+" as Italian writes it. Italian takes CLDR 42's `many` (the "1 milione" scale) as well as `one`/`other` — lint `MissingQuantity` said so on the first run and the generator writes it as `other`. **`TranslationCompletenessTest` is Stage C's gate**, the "gate both halves" §9 #19's optimisation required: for every `values-xx` on disk, the same string and plurals names both ways (a missing key would fall back to English on the phone, an extra one is dead), the same format-argument multiset per string (a dropped `%2$s` is a crash), `one` and `other` on every plurals, and **no value byte-identical to its English one outside a list of deliberate identities** — the list is the record of what is the same word in Italian, so an untranslated string cannot pass as translated. Plain text parsing, one file against another; lint's `MissingTranslation`/`ExtraTranslation` are the build's half of the same gate, live now that a second locale exists. Found on the way: the English delete-confirm text still promised "removes every reminder and all dose history" a day after R65 made delete an archive — fixed to say what archive does. **Device** (LE2123, Android 14, `cmd locale set-app-locales … it`): Today, Medicines, Settings and Appearance in Italian with no leak (the six palette names are pinned English, Appendix A); the picker lists Predefinita di sistema / English / Italiano (+ en-XA, ar-XB on debug); choosing English recreated the screen in English and the system per-app locale read `[en]`; the Appearance examples show 20:05 / 8:05 PM / 20:05 for the three time formats; reset to system afterwards. **Not done, by design:** MT-20's six date patterns (dates keep the locale skeletons); HM-33/34; a third language — which is now one `values-xx` folder, one entry in `AppLocale.SHIPPED`, and the gate; the emergency-card PDF's labels *are* in `strings.xml`, so it follows the app language while `CardLabels.ENGLISH` stays the test-pinned side (Stage A's decision (a)). **Left open:** Glance in the system language on 11–12; the `many` items duplicate `other` by construction; the Italian **wording** review on the device is the owner's — owed, along with the R59/R63/R65/R66 list. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **322 tests / 0 failures** (311 → 322), `lintDebug` green with the second locale (`MissingQuantity`, `AppBundleLocaleChanges`, `UseKtx` each fixed, none baselined); debug build installed |  |
| R68 | **§9 #29 platform hygiene — Appendix F.5 group 6, nine of ten items, to four answers asked first:** all four safety items (PIN fallback, missed-alarm self-diagnosis, unsaved-changes guard, delete-all + disclaimer) and all four platform items (OEM auto-start links, permissions section, AMOLED, start page) in; **MK-10 time-zone travel declined and recorded as a decision** — wall-clock is the right meaning of "the morning pill" on a medication app, and every reminder type but FixedTime is instant-based and unaffected; everything placed in the existing screens. Folded in: **HM-F1 decided as kept** (an expiry alert fires for an empty pack — the pack expires whether or not it is empty; the pinned test stands) and **E-16**, the backup coverage guard, after three migrations. | ✅ **Resolved.** **Piece 1** (`8390bde`): `BiometricAuthenticator` allows `BIOMETRIC_STRONG or DEVICE_CREDENTIAL` (no negative button — the platform forbids one with credentials); `availabilityOf` is the rule, pure: a strong biometric, else the phone's credential (`CREDENTIAL_ONLY`), else not enrolled, else fail open — the gate fails open only when there is nothing to fall back to, so a transient "can't tell" never strands a person outside Settings. The Settings row is enabled on a phone with a PIN and no enrolled finger, which is the owner's (the `it` dump of R67 said so in Italian). `unsavedChangesGuard` installs a `BackHandler` while a form is dirty and hands back the Cancel callback, so gesture and button both ask; the six reminder forms and the medicine form compute `dirty` from their own fields against the first composition (`rememberDirty`, re-based when the linked form's parent list arrives asynchronously). A non-dismissable disclaimer with an "I understand" checkbox precedes the lock on first launch — not a medical device, not the prescriber, a phone can miss a reminder. Found on the way: a "Couldn't save" literal in the medicine form that Stage A's gate had not seen (assigned to a `var` first, its stated blind spot) — now a resource, in both languages. **Piece 2** (`f396181`): `AlarmWatchdog` records every armed regular slot (`AlarmScheduler.schedule`/`cancel`) and every fire (the receiver, beside R65's last-seen stamp); on launch, *before* R61's silent repair, `verdict` — earliest planned instant more than a minute past, no fire since it, phone up five minutes: Daily Pill's two guards against AlarmManager's slack and a receiver that has not had its turn after boot — keeps one notice until dismissed: a card at the top of Today that opens Notifications & Alerts, and a line on that screen where the battery card's "may" becomes "did, at …". **Closes DP-F2**, the last open defect from the cross-app pass. `AutoStartSettings` resolves the OEM auto-start page from fourteen public component names (Xiaomi, Huawei, Honor, Oppo, Vivo, OnePlus, Samsung, Meizu, Asus), the packages declared in `<queries>` with a test that every one is; the battery card opens it when the phone has one. A permissions section on Notifications & Alerts — Notifications / Exact alarms / Full-screen alarms / Unrestricted battery — each a merged node with Allowed or Blocked as its `stateDescription` and a Fix button only when blocked, re-read on resume; the battery row keeps R39's generic list, not `REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` (the manifest's own decision); the three explanatory cards stay beneath. The test that caught nothing real: four rows emitted bare in `setContent` stack at the origin, so a tap on one row's Fix landed on another's — the rows live in a Column in the app and must in the test. **Piece 3** (`d4fb81b`): `ThemeMode.AMOLED` is the dark scheme on pure black with the container tiers re-derived from black, held to the same AA floors on all six palettes by `PaletteContrastTest`; dynamic colour stays out (it would bypass the ΔE guarantees, HM-34's own note). `StartPage` is read once before the NavHost composes so the graph is never re-rooted. `BackupRepository.purgeAll` is the restore's delete block extracted so the two cannot drift — every table, every reminder's alarm cancelled one by one (`rescheduleAll` only re-arms enabled ones), the projection dropped; settings, language and the lock stay, `EmivitaApp` recreates the default profile; behind the app lock and a checkbox naming what goes. **`BackupCoverageTest`** turned out stronger than planned: Room's annotations are not retained at runtime, so it reads the exported schema JSON for the current version — the artefact migrations are diffed against — and requires every table's exact column paths (`stockInfo.quantity`, embedded objects flattened as Room flattens them) to equal exactly one `BackupData` list's serializer descriptor, with no list left over; a column a migration adds and the serializer skips fails with the nearest candidate named. **Not done, by design:** MK-10; a typed-word purge confirmation (locale-dependent; checkbox + lock instead); dynamic colour. **Left open:** the watchdog cannot be provoked from the JVM beyond its rule — a real miss needs an OEM to kill the app; the disclaimer on a fresh install needs `pm clear` on the phone (not run: it wipes the owner's data). Emivita's composition root is a plain class, so the harness is the real one: `TestEmivitaApplication` *is* an `EmivitaApplication` (the receivers' `applicationContext as? EmivitaApplication` holds exactly as on the phone) with two members swapped — no native library, a container over unencrypted in-memory Room — and the receivers are reached the way AlarmManager and the shade reach them, by the armed PendingIntent's own saved intent, given a real `PendingResult` so `goAsync()` is honoured and its `finish()` (the `finally` block) is the completion signal the test waits on. Twenty-one cases in `core/scheduling/receivers/`: `scheduleReminder`, `noReminder`, `reminderNotificationNotActive`, `rescheduleReminderEvent`, `repeat`, `singleTaken`, `removeSingle` in Emivita's terms, and the untested paths — a disabled reminder's late alarm, the alert re-check against live stock (refilled: nothing posted, `lastAlertNotifiedEpochDay` untouched; still low: posted, stamped, re-armed), the pre-alarm while wanted and after, snooze arming and its re-fire, snooze on an answered dose, the lock's rejection of Skip, the three swipe meanings, an answer cancelling the pending nag, and the no-container drop under the stock Application. medTimer's `exactAlarms` is `AlarmSchedulerSlotTest`'s alarm-clock case already; its refill and location-snooze cases have no counterpart (no refill reminder type; GMS excluded). **Found by the harness, a production defect:** `SettingsRepository.useAlarmClock` was `dataStore.data.map { … }` with no `distinctUntilChanged`, and DataStore re-emits its whole preferences object on every write; `AppContainer.observeAlarmClockSetting` skips only the first emission and answers every later one with a full `rescheduleAll()` — so every settings write, the receiver's own last-seen stamp on every alarm fire included, re-armed every reminder on a background thread. Idempotent on the phone (same engine, same answer), pure waste plus watchdog churn, and in the harness a race that failed two tests one run in three. Fixed at the flow, where the intent lives; `AlarmClockSettingFlowTest` pins it and was falsified against the old code (`[false, false]` — the re-emission). **Piece 2** (`925f3be`): `MedTimerSimulatorParityTest`, the eleven cases on `SchedulingEngine.occurrencesFor` and `OccurrenceProjectionCache` with the parity tests' calendar. Six parities: `standard` (the merged projection interleaves two medicines day by day, untracked stock projects no run-out), `interval` (10 h crossing midnight), `intervalStartsFromProcessed` (Emivita's `assumeOutstandingResolved` anchors on the outstanding dose's due time, which is medTimer's reminded time — the same three slots), `windowedInterval`, `dailyReminders` (its `intervalStart` has no Emivita counterpart; the window is the anchor), `noReminders`. Four divergences pinned as Emivita's behaviour: **A-20** the overnight window projects nothing (medTimer five slots) and **A-24** the linked child is absent until its parent has a real event and then one slot per walk (medTimer 12 → 11 → 9 → 8 → 6; Emivita's run-out twelve days out on the parent alone) — the two §9 #31 flips, pinned first so the flip is a visible diff; **A-38** the walk does not read future real events; **A-46** an alert is not a projection entry, the run-out date is (three a day from twelve: 1970-01-04). `singleDays` not portable (A-13). | R71 | **§9 #32 database recovery — Lunar's `onCorruption` finding checked here; a different hole found and closed.** Lunar (`20271a2`) found that its `openOrRecover` never saw a corrupt file because Room's default callback inherits the framework's `DefaultDatabaseErrorHandler`, which deletes the file and reopens empty before any probe. Emivita's `EmivitaDatabase.build` was plain `Room.databaseBuilder(...).openHelperFactory(SupportOpenHelperFactory(passphrase))` — no probe, no set-aside, no recovery of any kind, and no spec paragraph claiming one. Read from `sqlcipher-android` 4.16.0's bytecode (`javap`): SQLCipher's `SupportHelper` passes `null` as the `DatabaseErrorHandler` and never calls Room's `Callback.onCorruption`, so Lunar's callback wrapper would be dead code here; and SQLCipher's own `DefaultDatabaseErrorHandler.onCorruption` returns without deleting when `hasCodec()` (a wrong passphrase looks like corruption, so it refuses to destroy the file). Net: on a corrupt `emivita.db` the file survived and the app **crashed at the first DAO call on every launch, with no route back**. | ✅ **Resolved** (one commit, code and record together, as asked), to two answers asked first: set-aside files beside the database, **kept forever, listed under Data & Backup** with one delete behind the lock; a **blocking dialog on launch, before the lock**, Restore from backup / Continue empty. `core/data/db/DatabaseRecovery.kt`: `openOrRecover` (Lunar's, the owner's own code — build, probe, close, set aside, rebuild, re-probe; a second failure rethrows), `DatabaseFiles.setAside` renaming the file and `-journal`/`-wal`/`-shm` under one stamp, `setAsideIncidents`/`deleteSetAsideFiles`, and `RecoveryNotice` — an on-disk marker, not a process flag, because the person may kill the app before answering and the next launch would open the empty database cleanly and forget to say anything. `EmivitaDatabase.open(context, name, factory)` takes the name and factory so the JVM test runs the production path on the framework factory (SQLCipher cannot load there) and the device test on the real SQLCipher factory with a throwaway name — the owner's file is never touched. **No production wrapper**, deliberately: `DatabaseRecoveryDeviceTest` on the LE2123 wrote garbage to `androidTest-recovery.db`, opened through the real factory and passphrase, and got `recovered == true`, the set-aside byte-for-byte equal, an empty count, and a fresh ciphertext file — the bytecode reading checked, not trusted. `DatabaseFileTest` pins Lunar's finding as a fact about the framework factory (garbage → `recovered == false`, no set-aside, bytes gone) and runs the recovery with Lunar's `KeepFileOnCorruptionFactory` kept test-side; a stale journal cannot be shown moving through an open, because SQLite discards an invalid hot journal itself — the rename is tested as the file operation it is. `DatabaseRecoveredDialog` before `AppLockGate`, gated on the marker read once the profile list has arrived (that read is what opened the database); Restore pushes Data & Backup onto the unchanged root once. `SetAsideFilesSection` under Delete everything, absent with no files, `incidents` injectable for its test. Strings in both languages. Verified: `EXIT=0`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **383 tests / 0 failures** (372 → 383), `lintDebug` green; device suite **7/0/0** (6 → 7); debug build installed. **Owed on the device:** the dialog itself on a corrupt file — the device test proves the path, not the screen, and provoking it on the owner's phone means corrupting the owner's file. Parking lot: salvaging rows from a set-aside file (same Keystore passphrase; a future "try to open it read-only") |  |

A-24: a linked follow-up was absent from the projection until its parent had actually fired, so Today, the widget and the stock run-out did not see it. A-41: the compute budget was checked once per medicine, so one pathological reminder dragged its medicine's others past it. A-45: the projection was published once at the end of a pass. | ✅ **Resolved.** Answers: **the day is the calendar day, 00:00–23:59** — an overnight slot at 02:00 belongs to the day it falls on, like a FixedTime at 02:00; and the follow-up is shown **as an estimate**, "≈ 09:00 · Estimated: 60 min after the 08:00 dose". **Piece 1** (`07786b9`): `windowSlotOnDay` adds a day to an end strictly before the start (medTimer's rule — equal start and end stays a one-slot window); the day loop starts a day back for an overnight window, since yesterday's may still be running; `DailyWindow.isOvernight`; the form says "Ends the next day at 06:00" instead of leaving the person to guess (`WindowEndsNextDayNote`, tested), and Save was never disabled for it. The R70 pins flip — with one fact worth its own sentence: asked at day 1 00:00, Emivita answers a slot *that night* (00:01 / 02:40) that medTimer does not, because medTimer's reminder carries `intervalStart` and did not exist the evening before, while a DailyWindow has no start anchor and fires the remaining slots of a window it finds itself inside — **A-06's documented rule, not an overnight defect**; the chain after it is parity, and both parity files say so. **Piece 2** (`788e1be`): `ProjectedOccurrence.estimated` + `afterEpochMillis`; the cache runs two passes per medicine — the reminders that compute their own slots, then the linked children: their one real slot anchored to the parent's most recent real event (unchanged), plus an estimate after every projected parent slot beyond it; ON_RESOLVED children estimate from the parent's slot too, the resolution time being unknown, which is what the "≈" is for. Parents are same-medicine (the form offers nothing else); one level (A-23). Today's node time takes "≈" when every entry at that instant is an estimate and the row says what it follows; the widget's time takes the "≈"; the medicines list only sorts by the next slot and shows nothing. Stock run-out counts the follow-ups: `linkedAndAmount` moves from 1970-01-12 to 1970-01-04, medTimer's 12 → 11 → 9 → 8 → 6. "Take now" stays available on an estimate. **Piece 3** (`3a6d398`): the budget check before every reminder's walk, and a trip keeps the reminders already walked — the first cut lost them by breaking out of the medicine loop before the medicine's slots were added, and the new test caught it with its own clock readings in the message; a partial answer, not a lost one. The state is published after each medicine with `ProjectionState.inProgress = true` and once complete without it (medTimer publishes at each day boundary); consumers reading an absence wait for the complete state, the rule `truncated` already set. **Found on the way, a real one:** the receiver harness (R70) leaked each test's container — the projection loop and the settings collectors kept running on a connection Robolectric had torn down, which in native SQLite mode is a JVM crash (`EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION` in `libandroid_runtime.dll` on `arch_disk_io_0`), not an exception; it had passed twice by ordering luck. `AppContainer.shutdown()` (test-only, `@VisibleForTesting`) cancels the scope and closes the database; the harness calls it from every `@After`. Answers: CodeQL and Scorecard now, dependency-review when there is a PR flow; the parallel release job in this batch; verification by actionlint plus a pinned-SHA check. **The files:** `codeql.yml` (java-kotlin; push, pull request, weekly; the private-repo permission pair; JDK 21 + SDK + Gradle as ci.yml has them; a manual `assembleDebug` so the Kotlin extractor sees the compile) and `scorecard.yml` (weekly, push, branch-protection rule; `publish_results` forced off on a private repository, the `repo_token` PAT line present and commented with where to get one) — both state in their header that the upload to code scanning **fails on a private repository without GitHub Advanced Security**, which is the expected result until the repository is public or GHAS is on, and the files are kept so that day needs no writing. `ci.yml` is two jobs with no dependency: `verify` (the §10 triad in *one* Gradle invocation with `--continue`, so a failing test still lints — R23's reports) and `release-gate` (`assembleRelease` + `tools/r8_check.py`, the APK as an artifact); the old one-job rationale was about not splitting the triad, which is still not split. `dependency-review.yml` not written: it fires only on pull requests and the ledger shows work landing on master directly — the one to add with the first PR. **The gate, `tools/workflow_check.py`:** every `uses:` pinned to a 40-char commit SHA with a `# vX.Y.Z` comment (all eight actions across the three files, resolved live from the tags rather than copied from medTimer — annotated tag objects dereferenced to the commit, `gradle/actions` and `codeql-action` being two); top-level `permissions:` present; with `--resolve`, each comment's tag must dereference to exactly that SHA on GitHub, and **every `with:` key must be an input of the action's own `action.yml` at that tag**; and actionlint 1.7.12 (a binary under `tools/bin/`, gitignored). **Falsified three ways** before the commit: an unpinned `@v4` → FAIL naming the line; a SHA off by one hex digit → FAIL naming the action and the SHA GitHub has; a misspelt input `java-versoin` → **actionlint let it through** — it validates inputs only for actions it knows by version tag, never for a SHA pin, which is exactly the pin this repository requires — so the input check was written into the gate itself, and the third falsification then failed as it should, listing the action's twenty declared inputs. | R76 | **§9 #36 four palettes, the reference app's colour grammar, Appearance live — decided with the owner before a line was written.** R75's pass stopped at the Stock slabs and the Appearance screen; the owner's verdict was that the six palettes were stock, the panels stark and blocky, and that nothing gets written until the palettes are mutually agreed. **How colour is applied, first:** four options were drawn on the app's own items from the five reference apps with screenshots on disk (HomeMedkit, MediTrak, Pill Time, Did I Take My Meds?, Daily Pill), the owner asked for medTimer — its eight listing screenshots were fetched — and its grammar was adopted as option E: every card tonal, the hue in controls and mostly as the *pale container* (state disc, Prendi chip, tab pill, FAB), solid primary only on the toggle and the rail, the medicine's own colour as one tile, warnings as glyphs. Attention became burnt orange, not amber, under the no-yellow rule. **Piece 1 (`b440cf2`):** `tools/palette_derive.py` derives every role from three agreed colours per register and prints both `Color.kt` and Appendix A; `--check` pins the appendix to the tool, `PaletteSpecConsistencyTest` pins the code to the appendix (17 roles, 136 hexes, both splash files — the night one had sat at `#1F150E`, matching no palette, since R56). | R80 | **R75 follow-up 1 — the Alarm tier rang once when the phone was unlocked; it rings until answered now.** Structural, not a bug in the loop: the loop lived in `AlarmActivity`, which Android launches from the full-screen intent only when the phone is locked or the display is off; unlocked, the system shows a heads-up instead, whose channel sound plays once. Decided with the owner: leaving the alarm screen unanswered keeps it ringing (it is the Alarm tier); the safety timeout is ten minutes, the phone alarm's own default, after which the sound stops and the notification stays. **Built:** `AlarmRinger` (the MediaPlayer-on-USAGE_ALARM loop and the 500/500 vibration, with the chosen tone and the two silent-switch settings, lifted out of the activity) owned by `AlarmRingService`, a foreground service of type `mediaPlayback` whose notification *is* the reminder's — posting an Alarm-tier reminder hands it the built notification and `startForeground` shows it, so the heads-up and the sound come from one place. It stops when the dose is answered anywhere (every dismissal now goes through `ReminderNotifications.dismiss`, which cancels and stops — five call sites cancelled directly before, and a foreground service's notification cannot be cancelled from outside it), and it polls the dose every 5 s so a path that does not cannot leave it ringing; the swipe action dismisses too. Started from the alarm-fired receiver: Android 12+ allows that (an exact alarm firing is on the exemption list — the phone logged `Background started FGS: Allowed … ALARM_MANAGER_WHILE_IDLE`); a device that refuses gets the notification with `FLAG_INSISTENT`, which loops the channel's tone. `FOREGROUND_SERVICE` and `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK` enter the manifest, whose comment had said no code wanted the former; it says why now. **Verified on the phone, unlocked, on the debug build with a seeded alarm:** fired 09:28:00, the alarm-stream player `state:started` at every 10 s sample for seventy seconds, the heads-up showing Prendi / Salta; Prendi from the heads-up → no player and no service within three seconds. `AlarmRingServiceTest` (four cases under the receiver harness): the post hands the notification to the service and a dismissal sends it a stop; the service runs foreground under the reminder's id and stops itself once the dose is answered elsewhere; unanswered for ten minutes it stops with the notification detached, not removed; a stop for another dose is ignored. The service measures its timeout with `elapsedRealtime`, which is also what Robolectric advances. Added to the list and to `<queries>` — and then found *exported but guarded* by `oplus.permission.OPLUS_COMPONENT_SAFE` (`am start` from shell: `SecurityException`; every other startup activity in that package the same), so `intentFor` now also requires that the resolved activity's `permission` be null or held: a button that throws is worse than no button, and on this phone there is correctly no button (`AutoStartSettingsTest`: found when open, skipped when guarded). | R82 | **A cleanup pass that became an audit: five review passes over the whole tree, the mechanical half made computable, and the first five fixes.** The owner asked for overbloated code, unnecessary comments, "AI slop", gaps and bugs removed, with the review and optimizer skills, and for modernization to be assessed. The bloat premise turned out to be half wrong and the gap premise badly understated. **Not slop:** zero `TODO`/`FIXME`/`XXX`/`HACK` in 119 files, and the 23% comment density (4,782 of 20,675 main lines) is mandated by §10 — the rationale comments *are* this ledger's working copy. The owner's ruling was therefore "only wrong or redundant ones, and cut the rest to 2-3 sentences max", which made comment *length* a computable rule rather than a matter of taste. **Tools (in `tools/`, commands in CLAUDE.md):** `code_health.py --check` exits 1 on comment blocks over three sentences, imports no longer used, and top-level declarations referenced only where they are declared; `--report` adds the rows needing judgement (duplicate blocks, oversized files); `--graph` emits one file's symbol-reference graph; `--selftest` proves each detector can still fire. Its first three runs were wrong in three different ways, each worth recording because each is a general trap: a `${...}` template span is live Kotlin, so blanking it with the literal made `EXPORT_FILE_STAMP` — used only inside an interpolated file name — look dead; a quote nested inside a template (`"${"%02d".format(h)}"`) mis-paired the scanner, blanked a real `*/`, and ran a three-line comment 296 lines to end of file, which is why the lexer now knows comments and literals in one pass; and 54 of 86 "unused" imports were `getValue`/`setValue`, which Kotlin resolves by *convention* for every `var x by remember { ... }` and never writes out, so `CONVENTION_IMPORTS` is exempt and a textual census must never judge them. The verification that "confirmed" those 86 used the same textual evidence as the finding, which is no verification at all; the compiler is the verifier, and it passed on the 29 the tool kept. **The optimizer, honestly:** both oversized files are a single connected component, so the structure does not collapse the partition problem and a search was warranted rather than assumed (`split_plan.py --explain/--greedy/--solve` scores a split by boundary-crossing references, `private` symbols needing widening to `internal`, lines over a 450-line cap, part count and size spread). `meta-optimizer` (engine self-test 78 passed, spec fully computable, 0 model turns) **lost to a greedy baseline on one file** — 336 against 161 on `SettingsDetailScreens` — because part labels are interchangeable and a population search has no gradient across symmetric plateaus; partition problems want local search. Adding move-and-swap refinement over every seed settled it: `SettingsDetailScreens` 538 → **136** (3 parts, 309/324/315 lines, 4 crossing references, **zero** visibility widenings) from the greedy-k3 seed, and `AddReminderScreen` 723 → **396.7** (3 parts, 361/323/449, 22 crossings, 7 widenings) from the *engine's* seed, which beat the greedy-seeded 427.7. So the engine earned its keep as a seed generator on the hub-shaped file and was wasted effort on the clean-splitting one. Both splits are computed and **not applied** — a 1,300-line diff in the same build as five behaviour fixes makes a regression unattributable. **Five review passes** (`dev-engineering-suite:android-auditor` plus four briefed agents over scheduling/alarms, the data layer, the Compose UI, and the test suite itself) returned ~60 findings, of which the ledger-worthy are: a **recovery overwrites the only off-device backup with an empty one** (`AutoSyncManager` had zero references to `RecoveryNotice`, and its swap did `delete()` then an unchecked `renameTo`, so a provider without `renameDocument` leaves the folder holding only `.new`, which nothing reads, while the screen looks healthy); a **"password-protected" export silently writing plaintext** (`pendingExportPassword` is a plain `remember` across the SAF activity, so a recreated activity wrote an unencrypted zip of the whole history and reported "Backup exported"); a **lost Keystore wrap key being an unrecoverable crash loop** that `openOrRecover` structurally cannot see, because `sqlcipherFactory(...)` is evaluated as an *argument* to `open(...)` and so runs outside the retry — found independently by two passes; `setAlarmClock` called without the `canScheduleExact()` gate its own KDoc says protects it — also found twice independently; medicine names reaching logcat in release with no `-assumenosideeffects`; and the privacy setting that masks names in every notification not reaching `AlarmActivity`, the one screen that shows over the lock screen. The test-suite pass found the sharpest thing in the batch: **the R81 gate was the weakest of the three sibling gates.** `LiveControlBesideLabelGateTest` had no lower bound on files scanned, so renaming `feature/` would turn it green over an unscanned tree where `HardcodedUiTextGateTest:107` asserts `sources.size > 50`; it checked `onCheckedChange = null` but never the `toggleable` row CLAUDE.md names as the other half, so deleting a row's modifier left an inert control and a passing build; it ignored `RadioButton` at 18 sites; and `args.contains("contentDescription")` was satisfied by `contentDescription = null`. All four closed, and widening it immediately failed twice in ways worth keeping: the negative lookahead had to move from `=\s*(?!null)` to `=(?!\s*null)` because `\s*` is free to match nothing and let `" null"` through, and `MedicinesScreen.kt:200` is *correct* code in a third shape — `RadioButton(onClick = null)` as a `DropdownMenuItem`'s `leadingIcon`, where the item's own `onClick` is the single node — so the rule was widened rather than given an exception. Five fixes landed: the auto-sync recovery guard, the kept `.prev` generation with both `renameTo` results checked, the export that refuses to downgrade (`backup_export_password_lost`, EN + IT), the widened gate, and 29 unused imports across 21 files. **One defect class, five instances, worth more than the instances:** `renameTo`'s discarded boolean, `openOutputStream`'s null, `resolveActivity`'s unchecked `permission`, `pendingExportPassword` going null, and `DocumentFile.renameTo` in the set-aside path — each a failure that returns `false` or `null` where nothing looked (§7.1 rule 4's shape, and rule 17's). **Modernization, assessed only, as asked:** AGP 8.13.2 / Kotlin 2.1.0 / compileSdk 36 are pinned with the reason written in `build.gradle.kts`, and `app/build.gradle.kts:264` records that compileSdk 37 needs AGP 9.x, which drops the `org.jetbrains.kotlin.android` plugin and forces a DSL and module-split migration. Mimirae and Lunar both moved to AGP 9.4 / Kotlin 2.4 / targetSdk 37 on 2026-10-11, so the pin is now a divergence from the siblings rather than the house default — but it remains a correct decision for a single-module app, and reversing it is a pass of its own, not a side effect of a cleanup. Robolectric stays at 4.14.1: 4.17 broke Room tests in Mimirae, so that bump gets verified, never assumed. | ✅ **Partly resolved; the rest is itemised, not forgotten.** Shipped: the five fixes above, both tools with a 14-case self-test, and the ledger-worthy findings recorded here. **Unverified by test and labelled as such (§7.1 rule 5):** the two `AutoSyncManager` guards — its write path is a `Channel` plus a 12 s `delay` launched on `Dispatchers.IO`, which virtual time cannot advance, so covering them needs a seam and that is a change in its own right. **Open for the owner, each a behaviour or wording decision rather than a gap:** `rememberSaveable` appears **twice** in 119 main files, so every form in the app loses its input on rotation (and `AppLockGate` re-challenges biometrics); drag-to-reorder on Medicines cancels itself the moment it works, because `pointerInput(index)` is keyed on the index the drag mutates, so only the TalkBack move actions function; a LinkedFollowing child is cancelled outright with no catch-up whenever its parent's alarm is delivered later than `delayMinutes`; `AlarmRingService` calls `startForeground` then unconditionally detaches, so the Alarm tier may ring from a background service — and `AlarmRingServiceTest:135` asserts `lastForegroundNotificationId`, a proxy Robolectric leaves set after `stopForeground`, so the test passes while the property is false; a snoozed dose is re-posted on the next launch, which for an Alarm-tier medicine means a full ring nobody asked for; and the Keystore-loss path, where the honest fix is not merely moving the factory inside `build` — a file set aside after the wrap key is gone can never be decrypted, so the recovery wording that promises recoverability would become false and needs the owner's words. Also held: the 172 over-cap comment blocks (2,456 lines) and the two computed splits. **Verified:** triad `EXIT=0`, **438 / 0 / 0 skipped** on fresh XML (the count is unchanged because the new assertions live inside `theGateItselfCanFail`, not in new test methods), lint clean, `code_health.py --selftest` 14/14, `--check` 0 unused imports and 0 dead declarations, `split_plan.py` scores reproducible from the committed graphs. The gate's first run **failed** on two real defects of its own before passing, which is the only reason its four closed holes can be claimed. Not run this pass: the release build and R8 gate, and anything on the device — the §9 #34 sitting is still owed and no night alarms fire on the owner's phone. |  |
| R83 | **§9 #38 settled with the owner and shipped: the six audit decisions, two new owner rules, both splits and the comment cap.** Asked first, in two rounds, because two answers added rules the questions had not offered. **(a) Rotation** (owner: keep input in every form): the six forms' fields are `rememberSaveable`; Stock & Expiration, Medical ID and Weekend Mode fill their fields from the store **once** (a saved `filled` flag), since their load re-runs after a restore and would overwrite the edit; the Advanced reminder section has a `Saver`, the linked form keeps the chosen parent **by id** (`Reminder` is not bundleable), a colour is saved as its ARGB (`NullableColorSaver`), and `rememberDirty`'s baseline is saveable too — otherwise the restored edits became the new "unchanged" and Back dropped them without asking. `AppLockGate` keeps its unlock across a configuration change (`relocksOn`: `ON_STOP` and not `isChangingConfigurations`); safe across a process death because a real stop writes "locked" before the state is saved. **(b)** `pointerInput(index)` → keyed on the medicine, with `rememberUpdatedState` for the index and callbacks; a cancelled drag now persists where the list already shows it. **(c) Owner's rule: a parent the phone held back rings as soon as it can, and its follow-up comes the set delay after the real ring.** The owner considered and dismissed a ±10-minute ring window. `DeliveryLedger` records the first delivery of each dose (fire or launch re-post; a nag or re-raise cannot move it), and the engine anchors an `ON_REMINDED` child on it when it is more than a minute past the slot, else on the slot, so on-time behaviour and the parity tests are unchanged; a re-post re-runs the schedule so children re-anchor. **And the app asks for every grant reminders depend on:** a one-time `PermissionSetupSheet` after the disclaimer and the lock, listing only what is missing, one Allow each (it replaces §9 #12's lone notification request; the system dialog is still shown once, later taps open settings); Today's `MissingPermissionsCard` when a grant goes missing later, "Not now" holding per set of missing grants; the battery exemption through the system's one-tap dialog, so the manifest declares `REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` — kept out since R39 on least-privilege grounds, a reasoning that holds for a store app and that the owner set aside for a sideloaded one (§1). Appendix F HM-37 is the same shape. Rows, checks and intents moved to `feature/permissions/` for Settings, the sheet and the card. **(d)** `onStartCommand` silences a previous dose with `stopSound()` instead of `stopForeground(DETACH)`. **(e)** `SnoozeLedger` (SharedPreferences, like `AlarmWatchdog`): the launch and boot re-post skips a dose inside its snooze, and both paths re-arm running snoozes — which also closes a gap nobody had listed: a reboot lost every snooze, since AlarmManager forgets them and nothing re-armed it. **(f) Owner's words: the data cannot be read, now or later; restore a backup — and warn ahead of time.** `PassphraseProvider` no longer generates a new wrap key over a stored one: a missing alias or an `AEADBadTagException` throws `DatabaseKeyLostException`, and `EmivitaDatabase.openGuardingKey` sets the file aside, resets the key, opens empty and records `RecoveryNotice.Reason.KEY_LOST`, whose dialog says so; other Keystore errors still throw, because deleting a key on a transient failure would destroy data a retry could open. (2) Scorecard on a private repository: the default token cannot run its GraphQL commit-history query ("Resource not accessible by integration"), a fine-grained PAT can. The owner made the repository public instead, so `scorecard.yml` is back to the upstream shape: publishing accepts a job whose steps are only the four approved actions, so the token-check `run:` step of `e0c0abc` would have failed it (`80ed672`). Published score 4.6 on 2026-10-12; the action's log line "Publication enabled: false" is a misleading print, the publish is decided elsewhere. (3) CodeQL's second run, on a warm cache, failed "could not process any of it": setup-gradle restored the compile outputs, every Kotlin task was FROM-CACHE, so the tracer saw no compile; the build runs `--no-build-cache` now (`d4bb365`). Code-scanning uploads switch themselves off if the repository is ever private again (`github.event.repository.private`). **Owner decisions:** GPL-3.0 (`LICENSE`); `SECURITY.md` routes reports through GitHub's private vulnerability reporting (enabled), so no contact is published; **no personal information in files or in git metadata**: every tracked file was checked (none), and the history was rewritten with `git filter-repo` to `haziaferi` and the GitHub no-reply address, every hash changing, this file's citations of its own commits remapped (`2c4da35`), the pre-rewrite history kept outside the repository as a bundle with the old-to-new commit map; Dependabot monthly and grouped for Gradle and Actions (`7969f77`); `master` takes changes only by pull request with four green checks (Build, test, lint; Release build, R8 gate; Analyze; Dependency review), no force-push, no bypass. Also: wrapper validation in CI (`686d572`). **Not now (owner):** a private release key. Release builds are still signed with the committed debug keystore, which is public now: anyone can sign an APK that installs as an update, and an update keeps the database and its Keystore key. This PR adds dependency review (the §9 #35 decision, "with the first PR"), which submits the Gradle graph first because GitHub cannot read Gradle files, and the **purpose map** at the owner's request: `docs/purpose-map.json`, rendered to `docs/purpose-map.md` by `tools/purpose_map.py`, checked in CI against the code (every route, settings destination, activity, widget, channel, top-level dialog, sheet and card) and against this file (every `§9 #N` and `Rn` it cites). Dependency review's first run needed the repository's dependency graph, which was off (the owner had it enabled, with Dependabot alerts). It then found advisories one library per run (Bouncy Castle, then Netty), so the whole submitted graph, 523 packages, was checked against GitHub's advisory database at once. Vulnerable, all reached only by AGP's tooling, lint, the tests or the device-test runner, never by the APK (`releaseRuntimeClasspath`): Bouncy Castle 1.78.1/1.79 (four advisories, two critical), Netty 4.1.93/4.1.110 (seven, one critical), protobuf 3.24.4, commons-compress 1.21, jose4j 0.9.5, jdom2 2.0.6. Each is raised to a patched release at least two weeks old by one table of versions, applied to the build classpath (root `build.gradle.kts`) and to every app configuration (`app/build.gradle.kts`); the two copies must stay equal. The release APK still verifies with the same certificate. **Open:** `kotlin-gradle-plugin` 2.1.0 has GHSA-r937-wjx7-w2jp (medium, unsafe deserialization in the Kotlin build cache), fixed from the stable Kotlin 2.4.20 (the advisory's text named a beta). Exported `11.json`; the migration list is now `Migrations.ALL`, which the database and the test both open through. **Readers:** `DoseEventWithMedicine.medicine` is nullable over a LEFT JOIN, and a row is the profile's when its medicine is or its `customProfileId` is (a row whose medicine is gone matches neither, as under the INNER JOIN); `displayName` and `doseUnit` give a one-off's name and no unit. The export's medicine filter lists medicines only. Stock, `getMostRecentForMedicine`, the PRN ceiling, the alarm, the reposter, Skip's lock check and `TakeAmountActivity` read `medicineId` and do nothing for a null one. **Adherence, decided here:** a one-off dose is **not counted**: History's percentage, its counts line, the chart and the PDF's subtitle count the list's medicines only (`countsTowardAdherence`), because a one-off was never planned, so it can be neither taken against the plan nor missed; it still shows as Taken in the list, the calendar's dots and the exports. It cannot become Missed: `reopen` refuses it and Return to due is disabled for it (no handler). Soft delete works as for any dose. Unchanged and noted, not decided: an as-needed medicine's doses do count toward the percentage today. **Backup format 3:** a one-off's `medicineId` is null, and a build at format 2 declares it a non-null `Long`, so decoding fails there with a serialization error rather than R60's refusal by name; `BACKUP_SCHEMA_VERSION` is 3, so such a build refuses the file as newer, as R105 did for the seen-alert statuses. `customName` and `customProfileId` travel in `DoseEvent`'s own serializer and default to null in older files. | R113 | **What R107–R112 left uncarried (gap review, 2026-10-22).** A read of the R107–R112 diff found nine places the new rules did not reach; each is a fix, not a decision. (1) Today's row Take and the dose sheet's Take now recorded the planned amount for a reminder that asks the amount (R109 B-45); they now open the amount screen the notification and the alarm open, materialising a projected slot first, and a projected entry carries the flag. (2) The widget's Take did the same; it now opens the amount screen too. (3) A broadcast Take from a notification posted before the flag was turned on read no flag; it is read now, as take-all's is (R110). (4) A sticky notification (R109 B-42) whose dose was deleted with its reminder or medicine could not be removed: Take and Skip threw, Snooze returned. Any action on a missing dose now dismisses its notification, and deleting a reminder or a medicine dismisses its due doses' notifications. (5) Answering in the app or the widget left the notification up, sticky ones for good; `DoseEventRepository.resolve` now dismisses it and refreshes the group summary through a `DoseNotificationDismisser` AppContainer hands the repositories, so every surface gets it. (6) Logging a one-off dose (R111) did not refresh the widgets (E-37, R104); it invalidates the projection as `logAdhocDose` does. (7) The amount screen's suffix was the stock unit; it is the dose unit. (8) A second Take while the amount screen was open was dropped (`singleTask`, no `onNewIntent`); it now shows the second dose, the first dose's notification staying up. (9) The PDF's adherence line said "last 30 days" for every exported range; This year and Everything are now named by the Export dialog's own range labels, with the counts in `history_counts_plain`, the counts line's existing wording without its day window. | ✅ **Resolved.** 18 new tests in `NotificationCarryThroughTest` (7), `TakeAmountActivityTest` (2), `ExportSubtitleTest` (3), `TodayAsksAmountTest` (3), `OneOffDoseWidgetRefreshTest` (1), `WidgetTakeAsksAmountTest` (2). Gate: 555 tests, 0 failures, 1 skipped; lint clean (`history_counts_plain` marks `PluralsCandidate` reviewed, its wording being the plural's); purpose map, code health, strings, palette and splash checks pass. Release build and R8 are left to CI. |  |

| R115 | **The R107–R113 phone check on the 6T (2026-10-23, 07:32–08:35) and the two defects it found.** Master 8a6337a's CI release build was installed over the owner's data (certificate 82548da9, data kept) and walked with a temporary medicine, the owner having approved placing widgets and exporting PDFs. **Passed:** Take on the notification of a reminder that asks the amount opens the amount screen (`startActivity`), the other's Take records (B-45); both reminders "stay until answered" are ongoing (B-42); a sticky reminder keeps Take, Snooze and Skip (B-10); a medicine with no colour of its own uses the palette accent (B-43, as designed); Today's row Take and the upcoming sheet's Take now on such a reminder open the amount screen, which shows the dose unit with stock tracking off, records the number entered and leaves the dose due on Cancel (R113-1, R113-7); "Take all (2)" and its sheet record both doses and leave no notification (D-20, R113-4), and a group with one dose that asks the amount shows no "Take all" (R110); deleting a reminder whose sticky notification is up removes it (R113-3); a one-off dose shows in History and Today and adds no adherence figure (C-15); the This year and Last 30 days PDFs name their range (R113-9). **Defect 1, widgets blank in every release build.** A placed widget drew nothing but a dot; the log said `WM-InputMerger: InstantiationException: OverwritingInputMerger has no zero argument constructor`. Glance draws through WorkManager's `SessionWorker`, and WorkManager creates the run's input merger by reflection; R8 had kept `androidx.work.OverwritingInputMerger` by name and stripped its constructor (dexdump: no direct methods). The app itself uses no WorkManager (§9 item 0), so only the widgets failed, and the Robolectric widget tests render without WorkManager, so nothing saw it. `proguard-rules.pro` now keeps `* extends androidx.work.InputMerger { public <init>(); }`, and `tools/r8_check.py` asserts the merger's and `SessionWorker`'s constructors in the DEX: run on the shipped APK it fails (`R8 stripped androidx.work.OverwritingInputMerger.<init>()V`), on this build it passes. Installed on the 6T, the widget draws ("Next doses / All caught up") and the log is clean. **Defect 2, answering one of two grouped reminders took the other's notification away.** After A was taken on Today, B, still due and sticky, had no notification. `updateGroupSummary` cancelled the summary when fewer than two doses showed, and cancelling a group's summary cancels its children, ongoing ones included. It predates R113 (a notification Take did the same). The summary now stays while the group has a child, counting one, and goes only when the group is empty; a lone reminder that never had company still gets none. `ReminderNotificationPolishTest` fails on the old code (`expected:<1 dose due> but was:<null>`); two tests that asserted "one dose showing needs no summary" now assert the summary stays, and the medicine-delete test compares dose notifications only. Robolectric's shadow does not cascade a summary's cancel, so the phone is the evidence for the cascade. **Found, for the owner (not changed here):** History calls a dose "Missed" the minute it fires, while it is still ringing and Today calls it due; the notification reads "Dose of 1 at 08:00" without the unit Today shows; the medicine page lists every reminder as "Fixed Time" with no time; deleting a reminder deletes every dose it ever had (`dose_events.reminderId` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`) though its dialog says "Its past doses stay in History"; the one-page PDF is 4.6 MB. A deleted reminder's nag alarm fires once into a no-op, as `RealSchedulingCoordinator.onReminderRemoved` documents. **Clean-up:** the medicine was deleted for good, the one-off record deleted, the PDFs removed, the widgets removed and the owner's moved icon put back; no Emivita notification left. | ✅ **Resolved** (defects 1 and 2). Gate: 555 tests, 0 failures, 1 skipped; lint clean; `assembleRelease` and `r8_check.py` pass; purpose map, code health, strings, palette and splash checks pass. |  |
| R116 | **R114's as-needed rule replaced by an *Al bisogno* switch (the owner's decision of 2026-10-23).** R114 read a medicine as as-needed when it had an as-needed limit; the owner rejected that, because a medicine taken as needed without a limit would move to *Senza promemoria* and lose its one-tap *Registra*. **The switch:** `Medicine.asNeeded` (schema 12, `MIGRATION_11_12`, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`), on the full form as a switch row *Al bisogno* (EN *As needed*) with *Si prende quando serve: resta in Oggi con il pulsante Registra* (EN *Taken when needed: stays on Today with its Log button*), just above the as-needed limit it goes with; the row is the toggleable node and the switch display-only. The quick-add sheet does not have it, since the sheet always creates a reminder. **Today:** a medicine with no enabled dosing reminder is under *Al bisogno* when the switch is on and under *Senza promemoria* otherwise; one whose dosing reminders are all paused stays under *Al bisogno* (R114's reasoning: it has a schedule). One with the switch on and a dosing reminder on shows its scheduled doses only, as a medicine with a limit always did: `standingSections` never listed a medicine with an enabled dosing reminder in either section, so nothing is duplicated. **The limit does not turn the switch on.** It is a ceiling on every taken dose (`getTakenSince`: TAKEN, any reminder or none), shown as headroom in Log dose, which takes any medicine; a scheduled medicine with a daily maximum has one without being as-needed, so the form saves the limit and the switch as set. **Existing data moves nowhere:** the migration turns the switch on for a medicine with no enabled dosing reminder (alert reminders schedule no dose) and at least one TAKEN dose; a refill or a seen alert is not a logged dose. A backup written before the field (no `asNeeded` key on a medicine) gets it by the same rule on decoding, since the decoded default (off) would move an old backup's as-needed medicines to *Senza promemoria*; a file with the key keeps what it says. The backup format stays 3: the field is additive, which an older build reads with `ignoreUnknownKeys` (the convention since §9 #26's notes; R105 and R111 bumped only for values an older build cannot decode). | ✅ **Resolved.** 14 new tests: `MigrationFrom11To12Test` (7: logged with no reminder on; never logged off, a limit notwithstanding; a dosing reminder on off; alert-only and logged on; paused and logged on; a refill alone off; fields kept and a new row off), `BackupAsNeededTest` (3), `AsNeededSwitchTest` (3), `TodayNoReminderTest` (1 more, the case table 3 more rows). Changed from R114: `TodayNoReminderTest`'s Paracetamol is as-needed by the switch, not a limit, and its case table now says a limit alone is *Senza promemoria*. With the behaviours undone (APIs kept) 9 of the four classes' 16 tests fail (11 failures over two runs), every behaviour at least once: the backfill, `expected:<true> but was:<false>` (3); the backup, `expected:<[true, false, false, true]> but was:<[false, false, false, false]>`; Today's rule, `expected:<[2, 3, 9]> but was:<[3, 7]>`, *Paracetamol, switch on, is under As needed* and the R114 screen test; the form's save, `expected:<true> but was:<false>`; the edit form's initial value, `ToggleableState = 'On'` failed; the row not toggleable, `could not find … ToggleableState is defined && … 'As needed'` (2). The 7 that pass are controls (never logged, a dosing reminder, a refill, fields kept, the round trip, a file saying off, a limit leaving the switch off). Gate (1 worker, low priority), after rebasing on R115: `assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest lintDebug`, **589 tests, 1 failure, 1 skipped** (the gated measuring run), lint clean; the one failure is `ReminderAlarmReceiverTest`'s *arms the next slot*, `expected:<[1792911600000]> but was:<[1792915200000]>`, which adds 24 hours to the fired slot and so fails on any day before a DST change (run on 2026-10-23, the clocks go back on 10-25); it touches nothing this row changes and is left to its own row. `purpose_map --check`, `code_health --selftest` and `--check`, `strings_review --check`, `palette_derive --check` and `splash_icon --check` PASS; `:app:assembleRelease` and `r8_check` PASS (2 widgets survived R8). Not yet walked on the phone. |  |
| R117 | **A scheduling test assumed a day is 24 hours.** `ReminderAlarmReceiverTest` "…arms the next slot" expected the next daily alarm at the fired time + 86 400 000 ms. On 2026-10-23, on a machine in Europe/Rome, the clocks go back on the 25th, so the next 08:00 is 25 hours away and the scheduler armed it correctly at 25 hours; the test failed (`expected:<[1792911600000]> but was:<[1792915200000]>`). CI runs in UTC, which has no clock change, so only a local run in a daylight-saving zone saw it, and only in the day before a change. The test now expects the same wall-clock time tomorrow in the system zone. | ✅ **Resolved.** Same test, passing on the date that failed; the gate line is the branch's (R116). |  |
| R118 | **Deleting a reminder keeps its doses (R115's finding, a fix of record fidelity).** `dose_events.reminderId` was `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so `ReminderRepository.delete` took every dose the reminder ever had, though its dialog says "Its past doses stay in History. A reminder that follows this one is switched off."; on the 6T a fired dose vanished from History with its reminder. **The FK is now `SET NULL`** (schema 13, `MIGRATION_12_13`, which rebuilds `dose_events` as R111's `MIGRATION_10_11` did, with Room's own v13 DDL; `13.json` exported, the migration in `Migrations.ALL`). **"Planned" is a column of its own:** R112's adherence read `reminderId != null` as "planned", and a deleted reminder's doses would have left adherence silently. `DoseEvent.planned` is backfilled `reminderId IS NOT NULL`, set by `materializeIfAbsent`, the one path that writes scheduled doses, and kept when reminderId goes null. Readers of the old meaning: changed, `countsTowardAdherence` (HistoryScreen.kt: the summary, the chart, the PDF subtitle) and `cardStateFor` (MedicineListState.kt, the medicine card's Due/Done); kept, because they need a live reminder rather than "was planned", `getPendingScheduledBetween` and `getAnsweredSlots` (DoseEventDao), `UnansweredDoseReposter`, Snooze in `ReminderActionReceiver`, `AlarmActivity` and `SnoozePickerActivity`, `reschedule`, `reopen` and `resolve`'s rescheduling (DoseEventRepository), History's and Today's re-post on reopen, and Today's projected-slot actions (`TodayScreen`, `TodayLayouts`). **Pending doses on delete (chosen from the code):** one whose time has passed stays, with no repeats left, and reads Missed (R119); one still to come is deleted, since nothing will fire it once the reminder's alarms are cancelled and it would otherwise turn Missed by itself later; a materialised future dose exists only after a reschedule. **Medicine "Delete for good"** still removes every dose: `deleteAllForMedicine` matches the denormalised `medicineId`, which scheduled doses carry, so it now removes alone what the FK used to cascade. **Backup:** `planned` and `missedAfterEpochMillis` are additive and the format stays 3, R116's convention; a file without them decodes `planned` from its own reminderId through the field's default (`planned = reminderId != null`), as the migration backfills it. | ✅ **Resolved.** New: `MigrationFrom12To13Test` (5: every field kept, the `planned` backfill, the deadline backfill, the FK `SET NULL` with the v13 indices, a reminder delete keeping its doses), `ReminderDeleteKeepsDosesTest` (4), `BackupPlannedDoseTest` (2). Changed: `MigrationFrom10To11Test`, which opens through the whole chain, now expects the reminders FK `SET NULL` and its "deleting the reminder still cascades" case keeps the doses. With each behaviour undone (APIs kept): the FK back to CASCADE, `expected:<TAKEN> but was:<null>` and `expected:<[1, 2, 3]> but was:<[]>`; adherence back to `reminderId != null`, `expected:<[50% of answered doses taken — 1 taken, 1 skipped, 1 missed, l]ast 30 days> but was:<[L]ast 30 days>`; the future pending dose kept, `expected:<[1, 2, 3]> but was:<[4, 3, 2, 1]>`; the past one's repeats kept, `its repeats went with the reminder expected:<null> but was:<1800001500000>`; `deleteAllForMedicine` not called, `expected:<[]> but was:<[1, 2, 3, 5]>` and `MedicineRepositoryTest`'s `expected:<0> but was:<2>`; no default for an old file, `expected:<[true, false, false, true]> but was:<[false, false, false, false]>`. Gate: see R120. |  |
| R119 | **"Missed" only after the reminders stop (the owner's decision of 2026-10-23).** History, its chart and counts, the PDF and the CSV called a pending dose Missed the moment it fired, while Today called it Due. **The owner's rule:** "A PENDING dose is Due while its notification is up or will repeat. It is Missed after the last repeat: fire time + delay × maxRepeats, from the reminder's `repeatOnUnanswered`, at the time it was set. With no repeat configured, it is missed at once. Missed doses count in adherence only from then on; due ones count neither as taken nor as missed." **Built:** the instant is stored on the dose, `DoseEvent.missedAfterEpochMillis` (schema 13, R118's migration; null reads as the slot), so it is the setting at the time it was set and needs no reminder. One function decides, `DoseEvent.isMissedAt(now)` / `isDueAt(now)`, and History's rows, table and calendar, the summary, the chart, the PDF subtitle and status column and the CSV all read it. **Written by:** the fire, `ReminderAlarmReceiver.handleAlarm`, as fire + delay × (repeats still owed), a snooze's or a move's fire included; each repeat, `handleNagFire`, which never moves it earlier, because Snooze leaves the armed repeat in place and it may fire inside the snooze; Snooze (`ReminderActionReceiver`, `AlarmActivity`, `SnoozePickerActivity`), as the snooze's fire + delay × (repeats still owed), from the stored `repeatCount`: the snooze's time itself lives only in `SnoozeLedger`'s preferences, which forget it once it fires, so it is copied at the moment it is set; `reschedule`, the same from the moved slot. A deleted reminder's past pending dose loses its instant and reads Missed from its slot (R118). The migration gives a pending dose of a repeating reminder slot + delay × maxRepeats under the reminder's present settings, the only ones on record. **Due on screen:** History's row says Today's own "Due" (`status_due`, no new string); the calendar has no dot for a due dose and its description counts it ("1 Due"); the History clock is re-read each minute. **The CSV** is data and stays English: a due dose is "Due", a missed one "Missed", by the same rule. **Not changed:** Today calls every pending dose Due, as it did; the widgets say nothing about missed doses; the launch re-post (`UnansweredDoseReposter`) puts a notification back without repeats and leaves the instant where it was; reopening a dose ("Return to due") keeps its old instant, so a dose reopened after its repeats reads Missed in History. | ✅ **Resolved.** New: `MissedRuleTest` (6, a fixed clock: no repeat; with repeats before and after the last; each repeat; snoozed; a deleted reminder; an answered dose), `DueUntilLastRepeatTest` (5: the fire, no repeat, a snooze, a move, a repeat inside a snooze), one test more each in `HistoryEntryRowTest`, `HistoryExporterCsvTest`, `ExportSubtitleTest` and `CalendarDayCellSemanticsTest`. Changed: the History functions take the clock (`HistoryEntryRow`, `HistoryTableView`, `adherenceSubtitle`, `buildCsv`, `buildPdf`), so `HistoryEntryRowTest`, `HistoryExporterCsvTest`, `ExportSubtitleTest` and `OneOffDoseTest` pass a fixed one; their Missed cases stay Missed, being past their slot with no repeats. With each behaviour undone (APIs kept): the fire writes nothing, `after the fire: null not in …` and `no repeat: null not in …`; Snooze writes nothing, `snoozed: null not in …` (2); the move writes nothing, `moved: 1792742672358 not in 1792746272382..1792746272382`; a repeat may move it earlier, `after the first repeat: 1792742143528 not in …` and `snoozed, then repeated: 1792741843909 not in …`; the label ignores Due, `could not find any node that satisfies: (StateDescription = 'Due')`; the CSV ignores it, `expected:<…[Due]…> but was:<…[Missed]…>`; missed counted by status, `expected:<…[0] missed…> but was:<…[1] missed…>`; the calendar's count, `ContentDescription = '[Tuesday 9 June, 1 Taken]'`. Gate: see R120. | R121 |

| R122 | **The History calendar marks an unanswered dose once it counts as missed (the owner, 2026-10-23).** R121 left the calendar without a dot for an unanswered dose at any age. Asked whether a day should show the missed dot once such a dose counts as missed in the figures, the owner chose "Dot after midnight". A pending dose past the midnight that ends its day (`isMissedInFiguresAt`, R121's one rule) now gives its day the attention-coloured dot master used for missed doses before R121; before that midnight it shows none. The day's spoken description names it Missed and the rest of its unanswered doses Due ("1 Taken, 1 Due, 1 Missed"), so the dot never carries state by colour alone (§7 R55); the rows themselves still read Due. | ✅ **Resolved.** `CalendarDayCellSemanticsTest` "an unanswered dose past its midnight is spoken as Missed" fails without the change (`ContentDescription contains '1 Taken, 1 Due, 1 Missed'` not found). Gate: 634 tests, 0 failures, 1 skipped; lint clean; purpose map, code health, strings, palette and splash checks pass. |  |
